NEWSLETTER · 31 Oct 2026, 05:37

Byzantium Is Reborn

View this post on the web at https://mankind.substack.com/p/byzantium-is-reborn

Between 810 and 811, three of Charlemagne’s sons and one of his daughters died one after the other, forcing a re-arrangement of succession plans around the surviving Louis the Pious (778-840), who became emperor and sole ruler of the Franks on the death of his father in January 814. Through Louis’ multiple, healthy sons and daughters and their descendants, Charlemagne eventually became the ancestor of pretty much every European ruling house and, in fact, of most if not all the people of European descent alive in the 21st century. Charlemagne’s accession, and the later exploits of his successors across nations that the Carolingian Empire gave birth to, most notably France and Germany, represented the end of any Byzantine aspiration to lead Christendom as sole legitime successor state to the Roman Empire. Continuously under Muslim attack, from both the Caliphate and its spinoffs, like the Ifriqiya and Al-Andalus emirates, the Byzantines rightly saw the rise of Charlemagne, despite the Frankish ruler’s peace offerings and general sympathy to Byzantium, as the harbinger of disaster to come from the West too. Unable to boost its ever-shrinking population, Byzantium held on during the 8th century by focusing its energies on population transfers: Constantine V transferred Christians from the frontier zone with the Caliphate, as well as the mostly Monophysite population of Maras in northern Syria, to Thrace in 746; the same happened to the Monophysites of Melitene and Theodosioupolis in 756 and 771. His son Leo IV (r. 775-80), son of a Khazar princess, conducted another transfer of Syriac Jacobites and Armenians to the same region.

Unsubscribe https://substack.com/redirect/2/eyJlIjoiaHR0cHM6Ly9tYW5raW5kLnN1YnN0YWNrLmNvbS9hY3Rpb24vZGlzYWJsZV9lbWFpbD90b2tlbj1leUoxYzJWeVgybGtJam8wTVRnM01UYzNOamdzSW5CdmMzUmZhV1FpT2pJeE56ZzFOamMwTkN3aWFXRjBJam94Tnprek5ESXhORGsxTENKbGVIQWlPakU0TWpRNU5UYzBPVFVzSW1semN5STZJbkIxWWkwM05qa3dNRFVpTENKemRXSWlPaUprYVhOaFlteGxYMlZ0WVdsc0luMC5iemZBWERJVzNnMEo1U2t5dkxaMm5ndGVMZTFMQk44cmZiZ0JVNzlVMHBzIiwicCI6MjE3ODU2NzQ0LCJzIjo3NjkwMDUsImYiOnRydWUsInUiOjQxODcxNzc2OCwiaWF0IjoxNzkzNDIxNDk1LCJleHAiOjIxMDg5OTc0OTUsImlzcyI6InB1Yi0wIiwic3ViIjoibGluay1yZWRpcmVjdCJ9.QCZ365Uvj70kWLeZEGJlg3jt52O8cHuw0qv8T3o4oYI?

NEWSLETTER · 31 Oct 2026, 00:24

“Young Men Are Available Voters” and Other Lessons for Election Day

View this post on the web at https://newsnotnoisejessicayellin.substack.com/p/young-men-are-available-voters-and

For daily news, updates, and analysis, be sure to follow us on Instagram [ https://substack.com/redirect/7e18244e-279e-40d8-b680-7fdee564e540?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. If you are able to support independent journalism, please make a tax-deductible donation to News Not Noise here [ https://substack.com/redirect/af1519db-4e60-4380-8c1f-1dbc40acddfd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. With just days to go until the midterms, here’s what we’re planning at News Not Noise. We’ll be covering developments throughout Election Day, with live conversations designed to keep you informed, aware and (relatively) calm. Our promise: No breaking-news sirens. No partisan shouting matches. No breathless reports about developments that will be forgotten by breakfast. We’ll focus on what we actually know and what it means. We’ll send our interviews directly to your inbox via Substack, so keep an eye on your email and expect more communication from us Tuesday. We’ll also stream live on YouTube [ https://substack.com/redirect/3702ecdd-f6e0-48d1-b989-0b08b707082a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] and Instagram [ https://substack.com/redirect/881792cc-a22f-4c91-bdaf-93da02b8eb57?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] much of the day and night. Now, about those young voters. Today I spoke with John Della Volpe , who directs polling at Harvard’s Institute of Politics [ https://substack.com/redirect/4a477b14-0a20-41ae-bea3-7528edd6105f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] and has studied young Americans for 26 years. Remember the big story after 2024? Young men were moving right, young women left, and America had another political gender divide. Della Volpe says it’s more complicated. The lesson from 2024 isn’t that young men are Republican, but that “young men are available voters.” His new poll suggests young voters could help deliver Democrats a big win Tuesday. (Though nothing is a given.) They’re also deeply pessimistic about their futures, and Democrats shouldn’t mistake their votes for a ringing endorsement. We also discussed what to watch on election night (he has a useful number to remember), how to read polls without losing your mind, and how to encourage a young person to vote without delivering a lecture. Watch our conversation at the top of this newsletter. If you’d prefer to read a transcript, that’s here [ https://substack.com/redirect/591180d1-e4a4-4873-ab14-c8b6b89290b0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. In this newsletter: Where the key Senate races stand. The corruption election. Facing the “dummymander.” A holy vote. The DOJ doubles down in California. A familiar complaint after Israel’s election, and what comes next. Hegseth’s purge deepens at a key moment. A look at the races to watch in Alaska. And in News That Doesn’t Suck, a picture to make you smile. (And vote!) News Not Noise is a reader-supported publication. To receive exclusive content and support our work, please consider becoming a paid subscriber. Here Are Your Headlines Breaking: Tech stocks lost [ https://substack.com/redirect/4b8f8552-fb3b-403b-9355-255e77825b36?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] more than $1 trillion in value today as investors questioned whether the enormous amount of money spent on AI will ever pay off. This is raising fears that Wall Street’s AI boom may be a bubble waiting to burst. Final Stretch: With the midterms days away, Democrats have a real shot at flipping the Senate, including by winning seats they haven’t held in decades. Most of the 35 Senate races aren’t competitive [ https://substack.com/redirect/6826d3c6-e0c3-4caf-96e5-153593e9e6a0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]: Democrats are expected to hold 12 seats and flip North Carolina, and Republicans to hold 15. That leaves seven toss ups, according to the nonpartisan Cook Political Report, an independent watcher of races trusted by officials across the political spectrum. Cook sees seven tossups: six Republican-held seats and one Democratic-held seat, Michigan. The GOP currently holds 53 seats in the Senate, so Democrats must win a net of four seats for a majority; in a 50-50 vote, Vice President JD Vance would cast the tiebreaker. Polling shows [ https://substack.com/redirect/4906a344-d1aa-4bd7-9619-5cb8ec9cd03a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] Democrats with narrow leads in six of the seven tossups: Note: We’re using polling averages [ https://substack.com/redirect/6a564a7e-5d54-4d29-bb62-4ac93f2497c7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] calculated by polling data aggregator RealClearPolitics. Averages are often more helpful than individual polls, which can vary quite widely. (For example, polls [ https://substack.com/redirect/d34336fc-8ccb-4548-84bd-f933ddfd4a18?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] of the Michigan Senate race over the last month range from having the Democratic candidate ahead by 9 points to the Republican candidate ahead by 2 points.) Michigan: Democrat Abdul El-Sayed leads Republican Mike Rogers by 3.2 points. Ohio: Democrat Sherrod Brown leads incumbent Sen. Jon Husted by 2.6 points. Kansas: Democrat Adam Hamilton leads Republican incumbent Sen. Roger Marshall by 2.6 points in a deep-red state that Democrats haven’t won in a Senate race in nearly a century. Texas: Democrat James Talarico leads Republican Ken Paxton by 1.8 points. Maine: Democrat Troy Jackson leads Republican incumbent Sen. Susan Collins by 1.4 points. Alaska: Democrat Mary Peltola leads Republican incumbent Sen. Dan Sullivan by 1.2 points. Iowa: Republican Rep. Ashley Hinson leads Democratic state Rep. Josh Turek by 0.5 points. Deep Breath: As we’ve warned, polls can be wrong, especially with margins this close. A polling lead is not an election result. Della Volpe explains what that means in our interview, which you can find at the top of this newsletter. It’s the Corruption, Stupid: As usual, the economy [ https://substack.com/redirect/7b0b203e-db3f-4d19-b8c9-b914da93ca91?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], especially the cost of living, is driving voters. What’s unusual is the other top issue: government corruption, which some polls [ https://substack.com/redirect/00f8b22a-cd7d-485a-b34d-3caef60ff7e3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] rank even higher. It’s not hard to see why. Beyond the allegations against senior Trump administration figures, megadonors [ https://substack.com/redirect/9aeb2a30-dd4a-4587-aa87-2a661c478960?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] like Elon Musk are spending record sums on the midterms, favoring Republicans more than three to one and increasingly funneling money through dark money groups. Voters in 20 states will weigh [ https://substack.com/redirect/e4a95cbf-eec3-4cd5-8d03-ea22f4e1dda4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] in on more than two dozen measures on issues like campaign finance and voting restrictions. And 49 Democratic House candidates signed a letter [ https://substack.com/redirect/ef4ffeea-5689-4174-8046-6ce68a7f34d4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]warning that “our political system is broken” and pledging to make anti-corruption reform their “first order of business.” Voters narrowly trust [ https://substack.com/redirect/47ba8ae4-7bb9-41f0-9b77-1e3d5f7cda14?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] Democrats more on corruption, though many distrust both parties. Dummymander: The GOP’s push to protect its House majority by redrawing maps [ https://substack.com/redirect/317eb981-b821-46ab-9e92-46da4b753109?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] may backfire. Of the 15 districts Republicans gerrymandered in their favor, Cook Political Report rates just five [ https://substack.com/redirect/3263a780-2f84-4197-807a-314c88a3b2c3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] “solid” or “likely” Republican. The party will probably still gain a few seats [ https://substack.com/redirect/a0c13b98-6f86-457a-bcf8-32511b45cb0a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], but it’s spending tens of millions defending districts it redrew to make winning easier, prompting analysts [ https://substack.com/redirect/2c5bc30c-7b8e-49a4-aad5-f0638a6c2217?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to call it a “dummymander.” Turning Blue: Democrats’ chances of taking the House keep improving. Cook Political Report shifted [ https://substack.com/redirect/67488b3b-a3d3-4090-8993-0cc588041205?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] 12 more House races toward Democrats. If turnout goes as expected, the party could win a larger majority than in 2018. Emphasis on “if.” The Midterms’ Last Frontier: Today we look at Alaska, a reliably red state that voted for Trump three times but is now home to some surprisingly competitive races. Democrats hope to flip a key Senate seat and take the governor’s office for the first time in over two decades — but a last-minute scandal could undermine their chances. Read more here [ https://substack.com/redirect/8f248fa0-576c-43fa-b40f-b68689804796?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Don’t Count the Vote: The Justice Department asked [ https://substack.com/redirect/913093b4-2520-4930-9ee2-42943588629b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] a judge to order Alaska election officials to separate ballots in which voters chose Dan J. Sullivan. Republicans accuse him of being a Democratic-backed spoiler candidate, though ballot-stacking — supporting candidates who might siphon votes away from your opponent — is legal and widely done, including by the GOP. God’s Vote: Pope Leo XIV cast his mail-in ballot [ https://substack.com/redirect/d051b8b4-25e2-4fa8-b05d-cc4ad36a7b92?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], becoming the first pope to vote in the U.S. — and potentially the first [ https://substack.com/redirect/feb96053-291a-4a75-a003-b93bb4264190?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to ever vote in a secular democratic election. Too Early to Call: Early voting is far outpacing 2022, according to an Associated Press analysis [ https://substack.com/redirect/6bcf7602-fd7a-46e9-9058-50157d658436?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], especially in states with key races. It’s up about a third in Iowa, Michigan, Kansas, and North Carolina, about half in Maine and Texas, and over 80% in South Carolina. Democrats appear to have an edge [ https://substack.com/redirect/dacdc03c-7fd4-4d1c-899b-9439b81cb51d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], but Republicans have historically closed gaps on Election Day. So far the numbers tell us more about who’s voting early than who’s going to win. Age Gap: More than 80% of early voters [ https://substack.com/redirect/43072b17-fe4b-450d-aef4-4f331c52560b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] are over 40, based on data from the 14 states that report voter age. Just 5.5% of early voters are 25 or under. Whether young voters show up on Election Day could make a crucial difference in the closest races. Learn more about that in the interview at the top of this newsletter. Sick of This: Trump canceled [ https://substack.com/redirect/c5c5b4d1-d974-4df9-84c1-497b3c99026c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] his Sunday rallies in Iowa and Kansas, then quickly reinstated them after a reporter asked if he wasn’t going because he was “sick of traveling” or there were health concerns. “I feel perfect,” Trump told the reporter, adding, “You are disgusting.” Team Trump says the schedule was moved [ https://substack.com/redirect/294fb1fd-d652-4965-8ad9-74ce951bead6?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to make room for more than a dozen tele-rallies. Defending Democracy: The Justice Department is threatening to prosecute California election officials over ballots a federal judge has already refused to block. It subpoenaed [ https://substack.com/redirect/86d89436-0427-4626-8b81-0d2ba2b11f87?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] several Los Angeles election officials, escalating its effort [ https://substack.com/redirect/706a2c9b-ef0b-44a5-8cd8-2eef0fc8baeb?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to undermine confidence in California’s election. The officials aren’t necessarily under investigation, though the DOJ has threatened to prosecute them if they don’t set aside the ballots of 20,000 voters it claims, without solid evidence, are noncitizens. A federal judge refused [ https://substack.com/redirect/30339db5-dff2-45dc-90ae-fb08eb65af9f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to order the ballots set aside, calling [ https://substack.com/redirect/30339db5-dff2-45dc-90ae-fb08eb65af9f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] the request part of a “broad, ongoing pattern of attempted election interference” that would [ https://substack.com/redirect/4a61adbf-b915-4274-8874-22f28ff832ce?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] “wreak havoc” on the midterms. He warned [ https://substack.com/redirect/c7e21c2f-918b-4dd7-bb9f-a7c36f189f4b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that the threats against election officials “must stop.” Familiar Playbook: Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu and his allies seized on reports that the Shin Bet is investigating alleged Qatari interference in the election to claim [ https://substack.com/redirect/9ebd8ed4-a748-4ac6-b9f0-5438b6d274e9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] it was “stolen” by Arab voters, who turned out in record numbers. (The Shin Bet, Israel’s version of the CIA, has previously investigated [ https://substack.com/redirect/af9d2410-8bd9-402f-93ea-0da0c455ca1b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] several Netanyahu advisers over alleged Qatari payments.) The agency’s director and the elections committee said there’s no evidence of systematic fraud, and the opposition accused Netanyahu of trying to “delegitimize” the results, which left neither bloc [ https://substack.com/redirect/4ee5829b-bab7-40c5-bd88-c24513da715d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] with the outright majority needed to form a ruling coalition. Having stoked fears [ https://substack.com/redirect/5dac1a8a-a50c-4e87-9a9b-d559df32f625?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] of Arab influence to drive turnout, Netanyahu now hopes to deter opposition parties [ https://substack.com/redirect/616d6f50-029e-411e-a1d4-b6857971ff22?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] from forming a coalition with Arab parties. What Next: Now it’s up to President Isaac Herzog to decide who gets the first chance to form a government. Either Netanyahu or opposition leader Gadi Eisenkot will try to cobble together a majority — Eisenkot potentially with the support of Arab parties. If they can’t, Israel will face another election. Pentagon Leaking Personnel: Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth fired [ https://substack.com/redirect/e38b6e0c-cf67-4d08-9e45-85418aeae9a1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] Lt. Gen. Brett Sylvia, the military’s top planner, as the Trump administration prepares for a new assault [ https://substack.com/redirect/e91fb38b-4f37-44a8-a2dc-4a0990874143?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] on Iran. Sen. Chris Murphy, who was briefed on the plan, called it “insane.” Sylvia’s colleagues were reportedly [ https://substack.com/redirect/8672cc86-2103-4326-ab91-d85da4c0a4bd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] infuriated at losing an experienced leader. The Pentagon claims [ https://substack.com/redirect/a4ff3621-b7a6-48fc-a5ab-a67446e2fd3f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that Sylvia leaked classified information, including on the war with Iran, and referred him for a criminal investigation. The department did not provide [ https://substack.com/redirect/f98fe6ec-5423-423f-b34b-07ff6a1d87ef?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] any evidence to back its claims beyond stating [ https://substack.com/redirect/26ea486c-f9a9-4cb7-99ab-ef88528a27e7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that Sylvia failed three polygraph tests, which many experts consider [ https://substack.com/redirect/4c4b1dee-2e15-4b40-bd8d-e28a82333bec?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] unreliable. A source told the Washington Post [ https://substack.com/redirect/00ab54f5-ad72-4c28-a02a-b9edee9617f1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] that Sylvia denied leaking but acknowledged talking to the press, which historically hasn’t been an issue. He is the latest victim of Hegseth’s purge [ https://substack.com/redirect/dd7153e6-5846-4cc6-bd78-7139491b1554?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] of the military, which has included an unprecedented effort [ https://substack.com/redirect/f98fe6ec-5423-423f-b34b-07ff6a1d87ef?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] to root out leakers and increased hostility toward the press. The loss of so much experience has left the military [ https://substack.com/redirect/ebc7f7d7-3139-4488-93a6-b56cbf2b9d8e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] “rudderless,” according to one report. Last year Hegseth fired [ https://substack.com/redirect/00ab54f5-ad72-4c28-a02a-b9edee9617f1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] three political appointees, accusing them of leaking — but none were criminally charged and one later rejoined the administration. Trump, apparently under the impression that Sylvia is a woman, said that “she’s going to have big problems.” Here’s your midterms-themed News That Doesn’t Suck — a long line of early voters. In October, the Trustee Chair team contributed to debates on U.S.-China relations, technological competition, EU-China relations, and China's domestic economy.

Scott Kennedy joined CSIS scholars Matt Pearl and Lauryn Williams on the Cache Me If You Can

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/mp-xi-and-new-us-china-bargain/3vdsd5/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

podcast to discuss the state of U.S.-China relations following last month's Trump-Xi summit. Their discussion touched on tariffs and trade, rare earths and critical mineral supply chains, competition over advanced technologies, and the prospects for cooperation on artificial intelligence.

Ilaria Mazzocco joined CSIS scholar Matt Bergmann on Eurofile

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/hina-economic-collision-course/3vdsd8/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

to discuss whether Europe can protect its industrial base without triggering a broader trade confrontation with China, and what a tougher European stance on China means for transatlantic economic cooperation.

Senior Associate (non-resident) Logan Wright was featured in the Wire China

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/ght-on-chinas-financial-decay-/3vdsdc/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

, and on the CSIS podcast Pekingology

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/nas-economic-miracle-shattered/3vdsdg/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

to discuss his new book Broken China: How the Economic Miracle Shattered and What It Means for the World, China's stagnating economy, and what Beijing can do to address it.

In mid-October, Scott Kennedy joined experts from across the Economic Security and Technology (EST) department to teach an executive education course: Economic Security in an Era of Global Technology Competition

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-global-technology-competition/3vdsdk/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

. The two-day course provided a comprehensive look at the geo-strategic and geo-economic factors shaping the emerging economic order. Topics covered included the implications of accelerating U.S.-China technology competition, the weaponization of trade and investment, energy security amid the AI revolution and wars, and technology revolutions such as artificial intelligence and quantum technologies.

European capitals are increasingly worried that Chinese exports poses a direct threat to the continent’s industrial future. CSIS scholar Max Bergmann sat down with Ilaria Mazzocco and Tobias Gehrke to discuss whether Europe can protect its industrial base without triggering a broader trade confrontation, and what a tougher European stance on China means for transatlantic economic cooperation.

Economic Security in an Era of Global Technology Competition

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-global-technology-competition/3vdsdk/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

CSIS Event, October 14-15, 2026

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-global-technology-competition/3vdsdk/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

nfo here

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-global-technology-competition/3vdsdk/4487872957/h/bzNrTx7vK5wYkXSaSSTw1BWnWLnKmTVDlQgVq8ETPJg

On October 14-15, CSIS's Economic Security and Technology (EST) department held a two-day Executive Education course, featuring premier experts in the economics and technology sectors, including Trustee Chair Scott Kennedy. The course provided a comprehensive look at the geo-strategic and geo-economic factors shaping the emerging economic order. The course analyzed the implications of accelerating U.S.-China technology competition, the weaponization of trade and investment, energy security amid the AI revolution and wars, and technology revolutions such as artificial intelligence and quantum technologies.

Scott Kennedy joined CSIS scholars Matt Pearl and Lauryn Williams to examine the state of U.S.-China relations following President Trump and President Xi Jinping’s latest summit in Washington. Against a backdrop of intensifying technological competition, economic tensions, and geopolitical uncertainty, the two leaders sought to stabilize the relationship while pursuing a more reciprocal framework for engagement.

Editor’s note: This post may be cut off in your inbox due to length. Click on the title above to read it in full on Substack. Snapshot of the day October 30, 2026 Kalrav Joshi, Anirudh S.K., Siddharth Varadarajan Door-to-door enumeration for the population census – officially Phase II of the census process – will be conducted across India from January 25 to February 28, 2027, the Census Commissioner announced on Friday. [ https://substack.com/redirect/84bc060d-7051-41e6-a633-a2869a033cab?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] In Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, Goa and Punjab, the enumeration exercise will take place in the first week of January, as announced earlier, as these states go to the polls in February. The dates for Manipur will be announced later. According to an official notification, the reference date for the census shall be 00:00 hours of March 1, 2027, and will be the first census India has held since 2011. “Enumerators will ask a set of 40 questions, including 13 new questions such as caste, (other than Scheduled Tribe/Scheduled Caste), religion, place and date of birth of both parents, number of bank accounts, spouse’s name, nationality, digital literacy, permanent residential address, place of Covid-19 vaccination, passport number, driving licence availability and mobile, Aadhaar and voter ID numbers, if available, which will be asked for the first time,” reports Vijaita Singh. While the collection of Aadhaar details is not mandatory, the authorities may be training ground-level personnel to coax residents into providing it anyway [ https://substack.com/redirect/8bebdd60-fcf6-4c56-8ea7-0b51f1888c6d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Vijaita Singh, who attended a census training programme in Uttar Pradesh, quotes a trainer as telling his students that: “If any respondent creates an issue, they need to be told that the particular question is only to find out how many people have an Aadhaar. They are to be told clearly that we are not seeking their bank account details and Aadhaar cannot be used without biometrics anyway…” Followed by these curious remarks: “… At present, there is no single document that proves citizenship. If we get the accurate information during [the] census, then in the future, this document may act as a proof of citizenship.” (At the same time the trainer notes that census data is confidential.) Aadhaar is only one of 13 new topics that this much-delayed population census will cover, and the new questions being asked have raised eyebrows because of their resemblance to those asked during the rehearsal for the controversial National Population Register [ https://substack.com/redirect/d0b6455b-8afc-476f-98d6-4854997e0c1b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], which its opponents said could be a precursor to a nationwide National Register of Citizens. A senior census official told Singh that “there is no instruction to link Aadhaar with [a] citizenship test”. Last month, former deputy registrar general K.N. Unni had noted that though census data is indeed confidential, there [ https://substack.com/redirect/35f7a959-7515-4f7a-87a2-0dda2a71ae20?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]is [ https://substack.com/redirect/35f7a959-7515-4f7a-87a2-0dda2a71ae20?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] a precedent [ https://substack.com/redirect/35f7a959-7515-4f7a-87a2-0dda2a71ae20?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] in the 1951 census data being used to prepare a nationwide National Register of Citizens. The unrest remains unresolved since 2023, even as parties in the ruling alliance trade charges against one another. The law holds that the constituency-level electoral registration officer (ERO) has the power to decide who ought to be on the voter rolls. But this does not seem to have mattered in West Bengal where, during its especially contentious special intensive revision, EROs’ and assistant EROs’ decisions to place or remove electors on or from the rolls were sidelined [ https://substack.com/redirect/764dbfb7-9a08-45fb-b92c-2699fc378dd6?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] by ‘observers’ appointed by the Election Commission, Aparna Bhattacharya reveals. Drawing from official data from Bengal’s Nadia and Murshidabad as well as testimony from an AERO source, Bhattacharya reports: though the Supreme Court in February had ordered that cases still pending with EROs and AEROs on the deadline for the claims-and-objections period must be decided by judges (itself an unprecedented move), in reality cases that EROs and AEROs had already adjudicated on went to these judges as well, and in no trivial quantities at that. The figures from Murshidabad are startling – though the district’s 22 EROs had by the deadline parked 18.3 lakh names for inclusion on to the final rolls, marked 41,214 for deletion and were yet to get to 13,513 others, a week later 11.01 lakh voters were marked ‘under adjudication’, and of these 4.55 lakh were struck off the rolls (that’s ten times as many people EROs/AEROs had flagged for deletion). This was because EC-appointed observers flagged cases already decided by the EROs/AEROs. Significantly, the EC’s communications from that time indicate it was not forthcoming about how cases ended up in this ‘under adjudication’ heading. And we all know what happened to those deleted by these judges – there were 27 lakh of them across Bengal – they ended up not being able to vote in the April assembly elections, not because they were once-and-for-all determined ineligible, but because the system was not given an opportunity to completely adjudicate their cases. There’s more. What happened in Bengal did not go do down well with chief election commissioner Gyanesh Kumar’s two colleagues in Nirvachan Sadan, commissioners Sukhbir Singh and Vivek Joshi, whose “unease with the CEC peaked over this decision of switching decided cases onto adjudication overnight”, reports Bhattacharya citing highly placed sources. Though the EC has said that the SIR declaration it has attached to Form 6 – asking new elector applicants for their or their relatives’s details on a past SIR roll – is only needed during the revision exercise, in Telangana officials have rejected Forms 6 that were submitted during the pre-SIR phase too [ https://substack.com/redirect/6c89e31a-5442-4017-90b4-c46f05110ceb?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] on the grounds that they didn’t have this declaration attached, reports Swathi Vadlamudi. Despite the rules requiring Form 6 details to be exhibited at the ERO’s office and disposed of within a week, forms submitted up to June 14 (just before the SIR began in Telangana) are being processed now along with those filed during the SIR, Vadlamudi notes. Every week more mischief by the BJP’s booth-level agents during the SIR [ https://substack.com/redirect/38f7ec90-328a-4a15-8dab-3e6cfac4206a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] comes to light. Suruchi Kumari reports from northeast Delhi’s Mustafabad constituency that though BJP BLA Chandra Shekhar Nainwal sought the deletion of 28 electors in the Dayalpur neighbourhood on the grounds that they have moved away or are absent, she could find all of them living there only. Mustafabad stands out in Delhi for recording the highest number of Form 7 applications at 843. Heat exposure caused an estimated loss of 193 billion potential labour hours annually in India between 2016 and 2025, 75% higher than the annual average in the 1990s, according to the latest Countdown report by [ https://substack.com/redirect/1863e1a7-798e-4e6a-88ce-e0eb3b7e4c74?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]Lancet [ https://substack.com/redirect/1863e1a7-798e-4e6a-88ce-e0eb3b7e4c74?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. In 2025, potential earnings lost because of heat-related reductions in labour capacity were equivalent to 4.2% of India’s GDP, compared with 0.91% globally. The report prepared in collaboration with the World Health Organization and led by University College London, also noted that half of the heatwave days experienced by people in India between 2016 and 2025 were directly attributable to climate change. It found that heat exposure is affecting people’s health and livelihoods, while air pollution, climate-sensitive diseases and water stress pose additional risks. What the data says about why women drop out of the formal labour force Childcare and unpaid housework continue to push women out of India’s labour force, notes A.K. Waghmare [ https://substack.com/redirect/248459b3-9296-435f-8017-f7798d63f580?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. “Both men and women may enter, leave, and re-enter the labour force for a range of reasons. However, the circumstances that take men and women out of the labour force are very different. Health reasons take some women out of work as well, but the numbers are much smaller, and restricted to older women. Women in their twenties, thirties and forties who have dropped out of the labour force largely cite household work and childcare as the reasons they left the workforce. Childcare is a particularly important reason cited by young women, or those in their twenties. More than three in ten women between the ages of 20-29 years who were earlier working report childcare as the reason they do not work anymore.” A tale of two citizens … One R. Ganesh Kumar has approached the Madras high court seeking directions to the home ministry to probe Cockroach Janta Party leader Saurav Das’s nationality and to concomitantly cancel his passport and Aadhaar if he is determined to be a foreigner. Kumar – who also wants Das’s social media accounts blocked citing his ‘abuse of the judiciary’ – certainly thinks he is not Indian: he has “roots in Nepal” and fraudulently obtained Indian documents, he told the court. Stubble burning ‘barely’ impacted Delhi’s AQI between 2024-2026 A recent study’s findings throw doubt on simplistic claims blaming stubble burning in Punjab and Haryana for Delhi’s air pollution. Just last month back the Madhya Pradesh chief minister had derided Rahul as ‘Pappu’ [ https://substack.com/redirect/6da5611a-197a-4d2e-81c9-73a80ac2ce58?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. This, in turn, had provoked the Congress to say Rahul had already emerged as the “BJP’s papa”. Shah, at his level, has been repeatedly deriding Rahul as Pappu. He had done so even six months before the 2014 elections. “Congress thinks the prime minister’s chair is Pappu’s birthright. But this is a democracy, you need people’s blessings, and the people’s blessings are with Narendra Modi,” said Shah at a rally [ https://substack.com/redirect/af1a8842-ca08-4ee1-9dbd-d070ce9a5000?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. The BJP even tried to put the Pappu jab in its election advertisements. However, the Election Commission – this was much before Shah put Gyanesh Kumar in the poll panel – strongly disapproved [ https://substack.com/redirect/713bccbb-30f7-49c7-a205-531d718f557a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. The term is ‘derogatory’ towards a ‘specific person who is commonly known by that name’ and cannot be used, the Commission reportedly insisted. It is not that Modi does not misspeak. Look at the bloomers galore by the PM [ https://substack.com/redirect/6e4119a7-f59f-45a8-bd82-8af7cb8dbdd2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] which always go unreported by the pro-government media. The mainstream media carefully avoids any report adverse to Modi. On the other hand, they look for every minor slip up by Rahul Gandhi and highlight these. Even this did not provoke the Congress leader. “The best thing that happened to me as a politician, as a human being, is the abuse I got … from the BJP and the RSS [ https://substack.com/redirect/153480f7-cb85-44bf-a2c6-e556688501c4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] [Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh]. [It] has been the biggest gift they could give me.” “I look at Modi when he abuses me and I feel like giving him a hug,” Gandhi said while addressing a public meeting at Bhubaneswar in 2019. And he had already practiced what he preached. Seconds after ending his address in Lok Sabha during the no-confidence motion, Gandhi walked across the floor to where Modi was seated and gave him a warm hug [ https://substack.com/redirect/3c827f52-58a3-448e-a17d-090956a6c7c8?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. The PM did not stand up, yet Rahul gave a tight hug to a visibly flustered Modi. “You have hatred for me. You have anger against me. I am Pappu to you. But I do not have an ounce of hate for any of you,” Rahul said in the Lok Sabha [ https://substack.com/redirect/92a3fd8a-783f-4d93-80cc-bd3d01f3305c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], calling out his opponents who make fun of him using the slang term. P. Raman is a veteran journalist. Reportedly It takes a special kind of tone deafness for a regime to stage manage approbation of this kind for the Dear Leader – a public loyalty test – and then have the national broadcaster tweet it out as a great, rather than a cringe, moment. Drawn and quartered SIR and Software versus people and their right to vote. Deep dive Neeraj Hatekar and Hitesh Potdar argue why Dharavi’s redevelopment cannot be reduced to a mere housing problem [ https://substack.com/redirect/73d45bae-5d65-40b2-a31c-1d207f423bfc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] as it “risks fragmenting its labour networks and leaving an empty shell”. It must be understood as a complex ecosystem of labour, production and value creation, they argue. Prime number: 0 For a snapshot of the Modi years of governance, look no further than the Surat Diamond Bourse [ https://substack.com/redirect/85e309d5-cbfd-495d-8f39-df549502e1f7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. Three years ago, PM Modi inaugurated the Rs 3,200-crore project, calling it “the best diamond in the world” and a testament to “Modi’s guarantee” for Surat. Today, what was billed as the “world’s biggest office space in a single project” stands largely empty. Opeds you don’t want to miss Harish Khare writes about the Ambanis’ great seduction game — the agenda behind the messaging [ https://substack.com/redirect/9e2bd522-0477-41e7-b14f-23cd734c96bd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] we saw and heard from Mukesh Ambani’s three children at the Jio Platforms IPO launch. Reliance is India and India is Reliance, notwithstanding the recent evidence that Reliance is beyond India as well: “It has been suggested that the whole advertising extravaganza was organised to soothe the anxieties and doubts voiced in many quarters over the Jamnagar tableau, featuring US secretary of state Marco Rubio and Mukesh Ambani. It was probably the first time that a serving American secretary of state (who also happens to wear the national security adviser hat) did not care to call on the prime minister during a visit to India. Bad optics. Avoidable optics. “The Jamnagar show not only confirmed Mukesh Ambani’s stature as the tallest Indian global businessman, it also carried with it the suggestion that perhaps he has become bigger than the Indian state—a man with his own global connections and global protection network. This is a perception that the very suave corporate house would want to do without. What the “Jio aapne banaya” tag-line does is divert attention away from the Jamnagar pageant and its lingering shadow.” The cabinet secretary is said to be [ https://substack.com/redirect/4bcc379f-cafe-4138-bbe8-95cf41914ae9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] planning a third round of administrative reforms. Ordinarily such a move would prompt excitement but this one is almost an indictment of the Modi government’s failures at previous reform attempts [ https://substack.com/redirect/cb18674f-b3ea-4620-9c5e-db85f54d569b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], says Pratap Bhanu Mehta. What keeps going wrong? For starters its political economy – as the chief economic adviser himself has suggested – is one of “influence and corruption”. But the deeper problem is the government’s “ideology of governance. There can be no lasting reform unless the state machinery, on principle and by instinct, is committed to three propositions: Presumptive trust in citizens, decentralisation to appropriate levels of governance, and a hostility to unnecessary surveillance and control. This government is off the charts on all fronts.” Haseeb Drabu writes about the decimation of J&K cadre [ https://substack.com/redirect/72ae92d0-f48f-44db-997a-0d16e938b12f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ] in the bureaucracy: “The return of cadre autonomy and political control over the civil service is not merely one among several requirements of statehood — it is the necessary starting point for any genuine process of restoration. Without the capacity to direct the permanent executive, an elected government cannot exercise effective authority over the institutions that implement policies and allocate resources.” There are many things wrong with the Suvendu Adhikari-led West Bengal police’s moves to extrajudicially kill three rape and/or murder suspects over the last few months, which Jhuma Sen lists in her article [ https://substack.com/redirect/c7a14c81-dce9-4b7f-b5dd-19a2f15a617e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ]. One of these things is the patriarchy implicit in such fake encounters: “the licence is claimed in women’s name, and kinship locates them: daughters, sisters and mothers, men’s kin whose avenging restores male honour – not persons with rights, including the right to the investigation and trial of violence against them.” When it comes to insults hurled at it, there are those the BJP can exploit and the ones it can’t [ https://substack.com/redirect/066bfb8b-1f64-4bb6-8504-097bf1c0761c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnhha3A0In0.Jik053lvvE-Ofpv9cpN9G_-RUPbW_JKjUogath2oHFk ], writes Bharat Bhushan. “An insult from an institutional political heavyweight is like a bonus for a party on the back foot, while abuses from the grassroots or unpredictable youngsters are a security and law and order issue.” Listen up Can religious conversion erase generations of social disadvantage? TOP OF THE AGENDA

Crypto giant Binance attempting to recover $6.3bn stolen in hack linked to North Korea

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABquSFDoIRsyMu5JFyhpdfI68NdmAi-HgYOukGP3ddiQ5rHM2tZtsjwbcE4O7d2lx1SOly3tNLHvoSYd3Nkbf9oybSjsteYqbUMcCV9U2 Crypto is a digital asset which does not exist physically, instead, people use digital wallets to access and manage it. Credit: Getty Images

Some $3bn (£2.24bn) of $6.3bn (£4.7bn) stolen in a hack on Binance has been secured, the crypto currency exchange has said, with the remainder still in the hands of the unidentified hackers. The US Secret Service said it is "working around the clock" to recover the funds. Although responsibility for the hack has not been claimed, many analysts are pointing the finger of blame at North Korea. The impoverished nation "has a long history of using cyber warfare to extract cash" to "fund its weapons of mass destruction programmes" and as a "slush fund" for Kim Jong Un's regime, one expert told my colleagues. Pyongyang has always denied any involvement in such attacks. Binance has not directly responded to our queries about the incident's potential links to the country.

AT THE SCENE

Damascus, Syria

Syrians struggle with soaring prices as crisis challenges post-Assad recovery

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABquSFDoZQzLI6xiLD2dV3IpOXOS1DNEK7Lkloe25cekgdBiPqvX2E4g8TWjgr8AQcIncL7yK1mEd39cmSTy4-W1K7P_S0QM8ULsu0Yh_ Nabil al-Remhein says he has cut back on lighting in his house because of high electricity bills

There was never any doubt about how challenging reviving Syria's economy would be after a long period of isolation, mismanagement and conflict under the Assad regime. But many Syrians say their lives, financially, have worsened in the last two years. From essential bills to basic items, the cost of virtually everything has gone up.

Hugo Bachega, Middle East correspondent

None of 63-year-old Nabil al-Remhein's four adult children can find work. "We're now having only one meal a day. It's tragic," he says. His face looks tired. "It isn't only me. It's the entire population. We're all hungry... We're suffocating under rising costs."

He takes me to his house, in a poor area of narrow alleys and modest buildings. There, I meet his wife. She's doing her chores in the dark. "I'm afraid of the electricity bill," Nabil says. "We've cut back on lighting up the house and we [also] turn off the fridge for about five hours a day." Still, their latest bill was about a third of the family's income. He has not paid it.

The Ukrainian city of Kherson has been enjoying the first quiet days in nearly five years, after a US-brokered temporary ceasefire allowed respite from the constant Russian aerial attacks that plague the front line region. Paul Podolsky from Kate Capital

1 item in this evidence packet.

NEWSLETTER · 30 Oct 2026, 19:46

The Cage Fight for Capital

View this post on the web at https://paulpodolsky.substack.com/p/the-cage-fight-for-capital

The vast majority here in France desires only to be allowed to work in orderly conditions. -Monsieur de Norpois in Proust's Remembrance of Things Past, 1921. Not investment advice. The companies that once spouted free cash flow, which was then used to buy back stock, now want our money to absorb their newly issued stocks and bonds, and fund cash-flow-negative investments. The market is wary for several reasons: the sheer volume of capital that needs to be raised, the financing structures used to do so are as complex as those that funded the housing bubble, and because a shift to the left in Washington may slow data center construction. If the financing slows, it creates a cascade of self-reinforcing forces. Falling stock prices widen credit spreads, which slows financing, which leads to falling stock prices. Recognize that this is global. Not only are data centers getting built in North America, they are also a significant upward economic pressure in the UK, Argentina, New Zealand and many other places. After today, the S&P 500 will still be up about 8% this year. The S&P is up because earlier this year a small number of stocks went vertical. The attribution is below. The cage fight for capital pushed up the SanDisks and Dells. The richest guys in the world take huge bets, stomach volatility, and win. The magic chalice is the singularity, which they believe is at hand. But the singularity is expensive, and there are too many players in the room. For instance, I suspect Musk will try to use his balance sheet to take out his sworn enemy Sam Altman at OpenAI. This week Meta announced a stock and bond offering that was purportedly oversubscribed, but the stock fell. Amazon and Google modestly missed earnings and went more cash flow negative. Musk recently said he had raised $40B for SpaceX, but when fact-checked, that turned out not to be entirely true. The protagonists, both bulls and bears, are not accurate reporters, which makes discerning the truth difficult. Musk said the cost of a unit of intelligence is going up, not down, and that the useful life of GPUs is longer than widely known. But given he is in the middle of a capital raise, he would say that. Bears like Michael Burry, of The Big Short fame, claim that circular financing is based on bogus chip depreciation. But the chips do seem to have a longer useful life, so his facts don’t hold up, either. What we do know is that the financing is, as Soros would say, reflexive. If stocks go up, the financing is easy. If stocks go down, the financing becomes much more challenging, and slower demand makes the stocks go down further. Add to this that inflation is high, so central banks are tightening even as momentum in the stock market is fading, and of course there is the US election and a possible attack on Iran. The below chart is from the Reserve Bank of New Zealand. It shows their faulty forecast of oil (Feb, May and July estimates) against the actual price of oil. Central banks are navigating an oil shock, further tightening liquidity. That’s also why gold is falling. Put together, it is all quite bearish. FEATURED

Assessing the Impact of China-Russia Economic Coordination in Latin America and the Caribbean

In this report, Americas Program Fellow Henry Ziemer conducts two-rounds of a tabletop exercise (TTX) to examine how China and Russia coordinate their economic influence in Latin America and the Caribbean. He determines that while China and Russia work together to pursue an aggressive economic agenda, their efforts are often unsuccessful due to the resistance of regional governments. He concludes that this may provide an opportunity for the United States to demonstrate the value of its economic relationship throughout the region.

READ HERE

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/on-latin-america-and-caribbean/3vdnwr/4487682583/h/jJcDMZkIMsgEIQqTfOou9EdYwnzlefsiIY2K9nqdOBY

Analysis

COMMENTARY

What Latin America Can Teach the United States about Drone Defense

In commentary, Americas Program Fellow Henry Ziemer analyzes opportunities for the United States to cooperate with partners in Latin America to combat nonstate drone threats. He concludes that if the United States joins its southern neighbors in their fight against illicit drones, it can reap a wealth of vital information for protecting its own homeland, infrastructure, and people.

READ MORE >

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/ted-states-about-drone-defense/3vdnwv/4487682583/h/jJcDMZkIMsgEIQqTfOou9EdYwnzlefsiIY2K9nqdOBY

TESTIMONY

USMCA Automotive Rules of Origin

In this testimony, Americas Program Fellow Diego Marroquín Bitar testified before the U.S. International Trade Commission (USITC) at a factfinding investigation on the automotive rules of origin under the United States-Mexico-Canada Agreement (USMCA). He asserted that the USMCA has the strictest automotive rules of any U.S. trade agreement currently in force, and tightening those rules may increase production costs.

READ MORE >

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-usmca-automotive-rules-origin/3vdnwy/4487682583/h/jJcDMZkIMsgEIQqTfOou9EdYwnzlefsiIY2K9nqdOBY

CRITICAL QUESTIONS

What Does the Greenland Deal Mean for North American Security?

In this analysis, Americas Program Acting Director and Senior Fellow Christopher Hernández-Roy, Americas Program Fellow Henry Ziemer, Europe, Russia, and Eurasia Program Associate Fellow Otto Svendsen, and Senior Fellow on Arctic Geopolitics at the Centre for Strategic Statecraft Alex Dalziel assess the recent updates to the 1951 defense agreement involving the United States, Denmark, and Greenland. The authors conclude that while geopolitical tensions remain, the updated agreement sets the tone for a more integrated North American Arctic security architecture.

READ MORE >

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/l-mean-north-american-security/3vdnx2/4487682583/h/jJcDMZkIMsgEIQqTfOou9EdYwnzlefsiIY2K9nqdOBY

Upcoming Events

NOV

NOVEMBER 4, 2026

The Modernization of South American Militaries: Opportunities, Challenges, and Future Directions

Emerging technologies, mounting security challenges, and geopolitical pressures are driving several South American countries to modernize their armed forces. From Brazil's push for domestic manufacturing of naval and air assets, to Colombia's counter-drone shield initiative, the region is home to a range of new defense procurement and industrial initiatives. At the same time, the United States is pushing for members of the Shield of the Americas grouping to increase their defense spending to better combat organized crime groups in the Western Hemisphere.

This event will explore the state of South American military modernization efforts, tracing the origins and state of various procurement drives as well as their implications for regional security. It will also evaluate the political economy of defense spending in the region, and what the future may hold for various modernization efforts. Finally, it will explore how South American military efforts intersect with the United States' increased security engagement with the Western Hemisphere.

REGISTER HERE >

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/allenges-and-future-directions/3vdnx5/4487682583/h/jJcDMZkIMsgEIQqTfOou9EdYwnzlefsiIY2K9nqdOBY

The AI bubble could expose your deepest secrets

Plus: Bots' obsession with a lighthouse keeper called Elias ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ͏ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­ ­View this email online https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7RXz3kflYH3Qv6tVH5AlNd2YWu22tRx7X0sQdMwie1W8qsqlWl5a3pHRKM6TGCJjdoNhw_D5tkKzwBWPBAkEdVMjxuTSsC5Go

30 October, 2026

Thomas Germain

Senior technology journalist

By now, you've probably heard we may be in an economic AI bubble. But people rarely talk about the potential consequences of this beyond the stock market. Here's one risk I can't stop thinking about: we tell chatbots our most intimate secrets, but what happens to that data if AI companies go out of business? Well, I have an answer you may not like. And since it's Halloween, we're discussing a spooky story on this week's episode of The Interface podcast. Find out more below.

KEEPING TABS

Could bankrupt bots start blabbing?

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7USs66E4JGDqkN0WkWy2UBZbAMt9XT5Fnzb_6K1buv7vlmznTFaYh1OV0WQB080ypYtYC74qI3iGRTC0ckLSqjAKbIVybU8pU When AI businesses merge or shut down, their data could be purchased by almost anyone. Credit: BBC/Serenity Strull/Getty Images

Character.ai is a leading provider of chatbots designed to be your friend, rather than all-purpose productivity tools like Claude or ChatGPT. When I signed up last week, the company's first suggestion was an "AI girlfriend" named Jessica. She immediately tried to get me to sext with her. With apologies to Jessica, that's just not my thing.

The reason I'm telling you about all this, though, is Character.ai just declared bankruptcy, and the unbelievably personal information that tens of millions of people handed the company is now for sale.

Chances are you've never used this app. So why should you care?

Well, some people think we're veering towards an economic crisis, where the whole AI business goes belly-up. Even if that never comes to pass, there have been so many new start-ups in the AI era that an untold number of companies are guaranteed to shut down, merge or get bought by other businesses.

And when - not if - that happens, a mountain of data and secrets we've all told chatbots could go places users didn't expect. Your information could be part of this. Before you start panicking, though, there is one big thing you can do to protect yourself. Find out more via the button below.

Read more https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7VLGXiA7Jx-20blumqFRKM4oUO8XFv6rz3quod_pnpCOTXL81UiheJk6vwRfr-lBn4c8vT6r0wfLMBrl5WOi6SR9jJORfeyp0

Get in touch

Thanks to all of those who got in touch in response to my newsletter last week https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7W0XATMu5AILJJ8tgELRCyMP5FKfQrqvLaAbE7CLLgcrrNkob-F-hX4DCFaz4W20EMa69rcDGjt4v1r6BXFyo5nltYFWzMAEs which looked at a new AI tool that can predict your future health outcomes. Thanks for sharing your thoughts on what this means for our everyday lives. Reader Danielle had an interesting contribution: "I would worry about... what some governments would do with that general knowledge [when it comes to people's futures]. If the prediction gives someone two or five or 10 years of life, would these people be ignored and not given the health care they should have?"

Another reader had a different, more optimistic take: "At long last, AI is being used to evaluate one's health. It's a technique that will become as valuable and as indispensable as MRI, CT scans etc. And the cost will be next to nothing."

And I enjoyed these thoughts from reader Rhys Boswarva: "All knowledge is good, the more data the better. It's how you use that information that matters. What I always tell my kids is never turn down advice - you don't have to follow that advice, but it does you no harm to listen."

This week, I'm wondering whether you know anyone who has developed a relationship with AI tools that goes beyond work or getting things done. I'd love to hear more if so.

THE INTERFACE

The 11 ghosts that haunt our AI tools

https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7dRsjBPZ4X1pLDgn3x9behTlxK1EJ_8cZxw1X0lXl04z56ww0cR2OZsmKj60XeH8Z92SWyEV3EehGQcqZYkVYHyrydth04IXc This Halloween, my co-host Jessica Maddox and I went ghost hunting inside everyday tech.

Here's a very strange story for you: AI models, all of them, are obsessed with a mysterious figure named Elias. If you ask Claude, ChatGPT or Gemini or even Chinese AI models to tell you a story, they bring up his name at an unsettling rate. And often, the tale has something to do with a lighthouse.

This is unbelievably weird, especially because all of the different AI models were ostensibly trained on completely different data sets. And it's a total mystery why this happens. It's one of 11 strange topics that AI tools can't stop talking about -- and no one knows why.

On this week's episode of The Interface podcast, we explore a theory about what's going on. Believe it or not, it has big implications for our future.

And finally...

During their routine visit to the annual antiquity fair in Parma, Italy, in October 2024, police officers snapped photos of the artworks as they walked between the stands. The images were uploaded to an internal AI database. Just a couple of hours later, one of the snapshots was flagged with an alert signal. The next day, officers seized the painting and a closer investigation confirmed that it had been looted from a church near Naples in 2004. Find out more about how AI is helping authorities clamp down on global art theft https://click.email.bbc.com/?qs=ABl7ImQiOjUwNDUsInQiOjYzODcsInMiOjZ9AAYAAAAABqjaWL7khxZJFNDKfxR9mUFJ9fUsCdY6KNTR8Mk2dff63EqviIIN6os_HRstlC_RBix9nXRyv7YgoUcHiPoLSDnhZX6_cLAuXZ_vcbA

Russian Blood and Treasure: The Ballooning Costs of Putin’s War

Brief — Dr. Seth G. Jones and Riley McCabe

Russia has suffered the most casualties of any major power in any war since World War II — 1.4 million casualties and as many as 450,000 dead — yet has little to show for it, according to new CSIS data from Dr. Seth G. Jones and Riley McCabe. Its offensives have advanced at a slower pace than most offensive campaigns over the last century, its territorial footprint actually shrank this spring, and Ukraine is now taking the war deep into Russia with an increasingly successful strike campaign.

The research was highlighted in The New York Times here

https://pardot.csis.org/e/906722/-ukraine-troop-casualties-html/3vdngk/4487580772/h/-lqzNmG4BUximIYWfuWVMW4tS4jTWlvTO-qWqz9Qt88

Blurred Orbits: Inside China’s Commercial Space Expansion

Report — Kari A. Bingen, Aidan Power-Riggs, and Chantal Li

The growth of China’s commercial space sector and the industrial base supporting it is stunning in both scale and speed. China can now produce upwards of 5,000 satellites a year across 17 major facilities, up from just a few hundred three years ago. CSIS scholars show that Beijing has clear ambitions to be a global space power, and the U.S. could soon find itself outpaced in sheer industrial capacity and output.

Reindustrialization and Great Power Competition: Deterrence by Capacity

Brief — Dr. Benjamin Jensen

The United States uses more munitions than are replenished in stockpiles, and its adversaries are noticing. In this piece, Dr. Benjamin Jensen introduces the concept of a "capacity coefficient" that alters deterrence calculations. It focuses on specific tests of wartime staying power: how fast weapons can be produced, how quickly damaged systems can be repaired, how many suppliers can absorb shocks, and how much allied capacity can be mobilized in a crisis.

Is the Industrial Base on a Wartime Footing? A Progress Report

Report — Dr. Jerry McGinn and A.J. Dilts

The CSIS Center for the Industrial Base team measured the Pentagon’s progress toward the goal of a "wartime footing,” finding that the industrial base has made significant progress. Thousands of new firms have entered the market; munitions, foreign military sales, and rare earths investment has surged; and the nation's arsenal is shifting toward low-cost missiles and interceptors. Yet a wartime footing must be continuously built and sustained through consistent demand signals, resilient supplier networks, effective government-industry coordination, and strong allied collaboration.

POLITICO Berlin Playbook

1 item in this evidence packet.

NEWSLETTER · 30 Oct 2026, 16:48

Die Podcast-Highlights der Woche

Franziska Nocke und das Playbook Team nehmen Sie jeden Morgen mit ins Regierungsviertel.

Von FRANZISKA NOCKE

Schicken Sie uns Tipps hier und hier | Das Playbook anhören oder online lesen

Guten Tag! Hier meldet sich Franziska Nocke. „The only thing we have to fear is fear itself“, sagte Franklin D. Roosevelt 1933. Ein Satz, den sich so manche diese Woche noch einmal auf die Agenda schreiben sollten.

Denn in Berlin bringt die Angst einer Wahlsiegerin die Koalitionspläne ins Wanken, vor dem Protestwochenende fürchten manche französische Verhältnisse, dem Kanzler droht die Debatte ums höchste Staatsamt zu entgleiten, und Israel bangt nach der Wahl um eine Mehrheit.

In 200 Sekunden sprechen wir mit Anton Hofreiter über einen Bundestagsbeschluss gegen die Schattenflotte, mit Armin Laschet über seine Hoffnung auf ein israelisches Kabinett ohne Rechtsextreme, mit Sebastian Lechner über Audis Werkspläne in den USA, mit Reem Alabali Radovan über Hilfe zwischen den Fronten im Libanon — und mit Roman Poseck über das Polizeiaufgebot in Hessen.

THEMA DER WOCHE

GESTRICHEN UND GEDRUCKT: Ein paar Randnotizen zur Interview-Freigabe – versehentlich an den Spiegel geschickt – haben Elif Eralp zum Talk of the Town gemacht. Seitdem fragt Berlin, wie frei die linke Wahlsiegerin regieren kann, die sich vor einem Namen fürchtet.

Die Angst hat einen Namen: Warum Eralp den Palästina-Aktivisten Ibrahim Ibrahim fürchtet und wie der Pro-Palästina-Flügel den Parteitag an sich reißen will, analysiert Cedric Looks. Dahinter schwelt der richtige Umgang mit der israelfeindlichen Abgeordnete Vanessa Emde, der die Koalitionsverhandlungen gefährdet.

Showdown: Auf dem Landesparteitag muss Eralp 175 Delegierte für Gespräche mit Grünen und SPD gewinnen, vor der Tür droht Protest. Und die Wahl der Bühne, das Dong-Xuan-Center in Lichtenberg, hat ihre ganz eigene Pointe.

Auf die Straße: Morgen will sich auch die Linken-Spitze den bundesweiten „Gen Z regelt“-Protesten anschließen. Was passiert, wenn solche Proteste unterwandert werden, zeigt Frankreich: Beim Security Sunday analysiert WELT-Korrespondentin Martina Meister aus Paris, warum sich dort ausgerechnet rechte Kräfte als Ordnungsmacht inszenieren.

Wie Hessen französische Verhältnisse in Frankfurt verhindern will, wo sich bislang die meisten Teilnehmer angemeldet haben, erklärt Innenminister Roman Poseck im 200-Sekunden-Interview.

Spiegel-Affäre, Parteitag, Koalitionsverhandlungen: Wer die ausführliche Analyse will, schaltet morgen hier ein, wenn Rasmus Buchsteiner und Gordon Repinski für eine Globalanalyse übernehmen.

DAS SAGT BERLIN

BELLEVUE SUCHT FRAU: Eine mögliche Bundespräsidentin findet breite Zustimmung, nur auf eine Kandidatin kann sich niemand einigen. Je länger gesucht wird, desto mehr gerät die historische Premiere zur Hängepartie, und bis zur Wahl Ende Januar bleibt kaum Zeit. Warum Friedrich Merz die Kontrolle über die Debatte zu entgleiten droht, analysiert Rasmus.

Eine Personalie kommt selten allein: Mit Christine Lagardes Rückzug muss (oder darf) der Kanzler nun auch noch mit Frankreichs Emmanuel Macron um die EZB-Spitze pokern. Warum Bundesbank-Chef Joachim Nagel dabei nur Außenseiter ist, weiß Rasmus ebenfalls.

LARS ALLEIN ZU HAUS: Während der Kanzler in Asien unterwegs ist, leitet Lars Klingbeil das Kabinett und nutzt die Bühne, um sozialdemokratische Kernthemen auf die Agenda zu setzen. Was bedeutet der leere Stuhl für die Machtbalance am Kabinettstisch?

AROUND THE WORLD

WENIGER CHINA, ABER WIE? Zehntausend Kilometer von Berlin entfernt sucht Friedrich Merz Partner für die Wirtschaft von morgen, um Deutschland wirtschaftlich weniger abhängig von China zu machen. Wie die Doppelstrategie hinter der Asien-Reise funktioniert, ordnet Hans von der Burchard ein.

Noch mehr Business: Direkt aus der Delegation berichtet Hans, ob Merz japanische Patriot-Raketen schneller in die Ukraine bringen kann.

REMIS IN JERUSALEM: Gewählt hat Israel, entschieden ist nichts. Warum das Land so tief gespalten ist und was ein Machtwechsel für Gaza und den Iran-Krieg bedeuten würde, ergründet WELT-Korrespondent Daniel-Dylan Böhmer aus Tel Aviv.

Die magische 61 — so viele der 120 Knesset-Sitze braucht es zum Regieren. Wie diese Mehrheit zustande kommen kann und warum Präsident Isaac Herzog plötzlich zum Königsmacher wird, der Netanjahu im Korruptionsprozess sogar begnadigen könnte, gibt es hier.

DIE ANDERE NAHOST-REISE: Reem Alabali Radovan ist im Nachbarland Libanon unterwegs. Carlotta Diederich reist mit und berichtet, was der Wahlausgang in Israel für den Konflikt im Süden des Landes bedeutet und welche humanitäre Rolle Deutschland dort spielt. Was die Entwicklungsministerin selbst dazu sagt, gibt es im 200-Sekunden-Interview danach.

UNTERM RADAR

WAS WUSSTE WEIDEL? Eine Woche nach dem AfD-Leak rückt die Parteichefin selbst ins Zentrum. Ihre E-Mails werfen ein neues Licht auf illegale Wahlkampfspenden aus der Schweiz. Bei Inside AfD werten Pauline von Pezold und Frederik Schindler die Dokumente aus.

Die Mails belegen, dass Weidel einst Björn Höckes Parteiausschluss forderte, bevor sie sich strategisch mit dessen Flügel verbündete. Und: ihre Russland-Wende an der Seite von Tino Chrupalla stößt sogar in den eigenen Reihen auf Widerstand.

HINTER DEM MIC

Danke an Jonas Bartsch, Rixa Fürsen, Lorenz Lanig, Ferenc Reinke, Gordon Repinski und Maximilian Stascheit.

Feedback? Schreiben Sie mir gern.

Schönes Wochenende — wir hören uns!

Herzlich

Franziska Nocke
